# v1.8 Root task queue

Root: Claude, since 2026-10-11 ([AGENTS.md section 13](../../../AGENTS.md)).
Implementers follow [WORKER-RULES](WORKER-RULES.md). This file lists the current
assignment of each task. Root updates it when it dispatches, merges or re-plans a task.

## Priorities

1. The critical path is the machine kernel, which unblocks the seven C16b and C16c
   machines (W01, then the machine tasks).
2. Content that does not depend on the kernel runs in parallel (W02, W03 and their
   successors).
3. Bug fixes run in parallel only when they do not slow 1 and 2.

On 2026-10-11 the owner chose to build the kernel as the accepted ADR-064 describes,
with Forge events. The proposed ADR-068 and the native/JDI observation work no longer
block any task.

## Active

Every implementer and reviewer runs as an independent process through
`D:/GitHub/ARCE-Task-Evidence/root-queue/launch_worker.ps1`. Their run directories are in
`D:/GitHub/ARCE-Task-Evidence/root-queue/<task>/run-*/`. A slice merges after its hosted
CI run and its independent review both pass. The reviews are kept in
`D:/GitHub/ARCE-Task-Evidence/root-queue/<task>-review/REVIEW.md`, and the merge commits
give their SHA-256 values.

| [W36](TASK-W36-JOINT-PROOF-FLAKE.md) | Parallel bug: diagnose and fix the joint-proof `SUPERSEDING_SAVE` flake (the first fix `4b116fae` did not hold) | Delivered `98035479` (Claude). Cause: vanilla's eager per-tick save (`ChunkMap.processUnloads`) of the freshly generated fixture chunk, which becomes saveable only when generation around it finishes, and which runs every tick once unpaced batches have put `nextTickTime` ahead of the clock. The barrier's refusal was correct; no product change. Fix `d88c1716`: the joint test waits for vanilla's own save of its chunk (`VanillaChunkSave`, 10-second cooldown) before its single attempt; 30 of 30 lead-batch runs, 10 of 10 escrow-only runs, full run 906 of 906 and the log checker pass. Root applied the same precondition to the other barrier test, `ownTerrainAndEntityStoresConfirmTheExactFrozenFrontier` (`e0bf3cde`); Root's loop: 10 of 10 lead-batch and 4 of 4 escrow-only runs. **Merged** `b6667e51`; shared CI 38448221067 |

Independent review (Codex) **BLOCK** (SHA-256 `cfd61b74…`): R1 High, a player who waits on the death screen (corpse removed with `KILLED`) skips the check; R2 High, a cancelled or interrupted restore erases the saved point; R3 High, a vetoed clear lets a refused destination through; R4 Medium, the bed-setter token is not operation-scoped; R5 Medium, sleep admission trusts `hasChunkAt`. [W37F](TASK-W37F-REVIEW-FIXES.md) (Codex) delivered `3764b2e6`: decide on each dead-player tick, keep the `DISCARDED` check, clear and restore by access-transformer field writes with a durable `PlayerPersisted` backup, the setter cancel removed (D1 outcome 3 moves to W37b), completed-chunk checks; 2,978 unit tests; all W37 GameTests pass, but two W38 player-fall GameTests fail on the branch after its shared merge (they pass on shared). [W37G](TASK-W37G.md) (Codex) found the cause, `fd4b1324`: `ServerPlayer.doCheckFallDamage` returns early while the player's inflated box touches an unloaded chunk, and W38's `landing` fixture loaded only its own chunk, so the result depended on where the batch runner placed the test. Root fix on shared `a39ecbec` loads the 3 × 3 neighbourhood; merged into the W37 branch as `f8f75ba3`, branch CI 38492880946. Independent re-review of `3764b2e6` (Claude) **BLOCK** (SHA-256 `bc7d5b2b…`): F1 High, a point set while a backup is pending (`/spawnpoint` on a dead player, or another mod's respawn setter) is overwritten by the older backup; F2 Medium, the End exit is not judged; F3 to F8 Low (freshness premise, `doTick`'s spectator guard, no diagnostics on fail-open cases, enum-name tokens, per-tick rewrite, `loadedVent` scope). [W37H](TASK-W37H.md) (Claude, since 09:04): a restore writes only into W37F's exact cleared tuple and any other tuple or an observed foreign setter supersedes the backup; the End exit is judged; the decision moves to `ServerTickEvent` END at LOWEST; one WARN per player per session for the fail-open cases; pinned outcome tokens; no per-tick rewrite; F8 accepted. W37H delivered `e8880994` (992 of 992 GameTests). Root then narrowed F1: a backup is superseded only when the native tuple differs from the cleared tuple, because counting every foreign setter loses the point when another mod's `Clone` listener copies the cleared tuple. [W37I](TASK-W37I.md) (Claude) delivered `9649c9ed`: tuple-only supersession, and a newer point on an unadopted replacement is copied to the old player before its logout save; 995 of 995 GameTests. Root merged shared into the branch (`a7b2829b`, language files merged three-way), branch CI 38516997646. Branch CI 38516997646 passed at `a7b2829b`. The Codex re-review of `d92216d9..9649c9ed` **BLOCK** (SHA-256 `eaeb57cf…`): F1 High, in the interrupted-listener window a newer point is lost when logout comes before the copy, or when a second setter follows it; F2 Medium, a cleared replacement resurrects a backup already superseded on the old player when the native setter is vetoed or no arrival is found; F3 Low, risk text. [W37J](TASK-W37J.md) (Codex) delivered `07fcf73c` (3,110 unit tests, 1,045 GameTests); Root review **MERGE** (SHA-256 `e01e344d…`); shared merged (`de1935ae`), branch CI 38530882792 passed. **Merged** `4f9e6597` with the ADR-066 W37 note, R-036 and the two config ledger rows; `sleepEvent` waits for W37b. W37J scope: judge supersession from `Clone`'s original player, and reconcile the unadopted old player from the replacement's latest tuple. Its first stop showed `SaveToFile` fires after the `.dat` write, so amendment 1 moved the boundary to `PlayerLoggedOutEvent` (posted before `PlayerList.remove` saves); its second stop showed server stop also saves the old player through `onDisconnect`, so amendment 2 treats shutdown as the same logout path. Resumed 14:35. Then a fresh Claude review. Root's answers to its questions: (1) the `natural=true` type change is a separate scoped task, W37b, after W37 merges: one v1.8 writer per type, Root's `build.gradle` exclusion, and the automated D1-V01 to D1-V13 rows (real clients in the final campaign); until then vanilla still refuses off-world beds. (2) No change: sleeping off-world keeps the existing point (D1 outcome 3, owner decision 03), so `allowPlanetRespawn` applies to points set in other ways. A different behaviour needs an owner decision |

Part B ([TASK-W11B2-FINAL-REVIEW-B](TASK-W11B2-FINAL-REVIEW-B.md), Claude, on the review tree `12f5a5b2`: the W11b2 branch at `3b35a8af` with the C41 branch's `a28004df` merged) returned **FIX-FIRST** (`W11b2-final-review-B/REVIEW.md`, SHA-256 `60581547…`; 188 unit tests and 44 runner tests rerun). The flight state machine matches design 5.4 on uncrewed and always-seated paths, but ordinary player actions put crewed flights into permanent, silent holds. F1 High: the frozen E includes the live rider list, so a dismount, logout or restart after F1 stops F1 to F4 and the pre-F2 cancellation. F2 High: since `f8095b08` nothing re-seats an assigned passenger at rest, so a dismount after landing or a reconnect leaves the F6 wait open for ever. F3 to F6 Medium: the healthy descent rehashes complete envelopes many times per tick; the journal-wide lease and all-or-nothing batches fail healthy flights under unrelated journal activity; guard refusals count as writer failures and three make the writer unavailable; harmless knockback sets sticky holds. F7 to F10 Low (no crewed restart evidence, gate wiring tests, a misleading F2 message, cubic retention). No native cut had a mounted passenger. Root decision **R17** (`W11b2-C46/ROOT-DECISION-R17.md`, SHA-256 `c31254f8…`): live riders are compared as F5 already does (a duplicate-free subset of the assigned manifest), not frozen in E; an online assigned passenger at rest falls through to F5's move, and CANCELLED needs each assigned passenger online, not riding another vehicle and in the source's Level; the checked journal lease is per logical rocket, and a batch is captured again without a failing request; a guard refusal does not count towards writer unavailability; motion the entity's own tick zeroes is transient; non-transient executor holds log one WARN and stop refreshing tickets. Only identifiers grow (an omitted namespace adds `minecraft:`, +10 characters). Only asteroid and gas missions can cross their 4,096-byte bound, by up to 200 bytes, and the whole root can cross 16 MiB even when every record fits. This mod never wrote such names, so only an edited or foreign file contains them. Such a root loads but can never be written, and each session's changes are lost at the next start; with an unrelated load repair the world refuses to start, with no reason logged. Recommendation, option A: re-encode every loaded root (106 ms at the bound) and refuse an unwritable one under R-030 with a reason naming the record. That needs the owner, because such worlds would stop starting (owner question D15). Separately, three content parsers bound the spelling, not the canonical ID, so a datapack satellite definition with a 138-character canonical ID saves and then blocks the next start: that is W177 with W168-R5's guards. The answer is a qualified yes. Automatic pad charging can be made crash-safe with merged mechanisms only (no new hook, Access Transformer or Mixin) for every player whose data loads from `playerdata`. The protocol is a debit and outbox in the pad's chunk, a terrain proof, then the credit and a receipt in `ForgeData.PlayerPersisted`. W80's prompt save and streaming readback follow, then the acknowledgement by a second terrain proof. The single-player host loads from `level.dat` and is refused. Section 9 gives the owner O1 (recommended: the pad fills canisters, plus a canister-to-tank refill) or O2 (automatic charging labelled not crash-safe). (the Claude quota is close to its limit; Root steps back to coordination and Codex does the work). - 2026-10-22: W122 found that the heavy-JVM lock example in WORKER-RULES was unsafe. `New-Item -ItemType Directory` let W122's runner and W128's benchmark both take the lock at 22:09:45.987 on 2026-10-21, and W128's unchecked `finally` then removed the lock while W122's run still held it. Root changed the example to an atomic `cmd /c mkdir` and a `finally` that removes the lock only while `owner.txt` still holds the script's own token. Workers started before this change (W125F, W131 and W132) may still use the old pattern, so Root checks `owner.txt` and the Java processes while they run.
- 2026-10-22: W132F waited a long time for the heavy-JVM lock because W133's runner took it again within seconds of each release between its native cases, while every other runner polls once a minute. Root added a hand-off rule to WORKER-RULES: a script that takes the lock several times in a row waits at least 90 seconds after each release.
- 2026-10-22: the hosted regression run on `339dd2a1` (W132's merge) failed only in the GameTest log check: its GameTests ran from 23:37 to 00:13 UTC and Forge's log4j configuration rolled `latest.log` at midnight, so the checker saw no launch header and zero counts while all 1,298 tests passed. The runs on `2c1c32f7` and `eeb1fb80` contain the same code and started after midnight. Root queued W135 for the checker.

Their worktrees and commits were intact and no lock was held. Root resumed each in its own session at 14:49 (`codex-resume` for W136 and W133F, `claude-resume` for W130F).
- 2026-10-22 19:45, **Root decision R64** on the owner's message in the Claude Code session that day: "周限快到了，没你把握全局不行啊，是时候你少干点，让codex多干点了..." (Claude's weekly limit is close; Claude stays Root over the whole picture but does less, and Codex does more). Until the owner says the limit has reset: every new worker session, implementer or reviewer, is Codex (at most two at a time, as before); an independent review of Codex work is a fresh Codex session with no context from the author; Root reads each worker's report and diff summary, relies on hosted CI and the reviews, and reruns locally only for persistence or production changes; Claude sessions already running finish their tasks. W142 was stopped and requeued for Codex.
